And there is one special thing about this legislation: it is based on certain very specific rules. One particular rule for us is the Constitution. The Constitution is the ultimate law. It is probably not even necessary to be a lawyer, because probably all people have heard that no law, no regulation, no other document, must be an act contrary to the Constitution. Despite the fact that we in this Riigikogu for days are adopting directives and regulations coming from Brussels and Strasbourg, without any understanding of what is going to happen there, we are adopting them on a massive scale, we still believe in the Constitution itself. Recently, it was the 100th anniversary of the Constitution. You know how good it is to hear how good this constitution is, how unwavering it is. Nothing has changed about him, he's like an iron rod around us, protecting us from all bad legal regulations. Now I tell you, good friends, it's wrong too. Our constitution has been shot in the hole, full of holes, wormed. And this has not been done by the good people who adopted it in 1992, or by the Constitutional Assembly, who approved or drafted our constitution, they did a very good law. But these later, these judges, the creators of justice, who have been sitting here in this Chamber, have chewed this constitution with holes in the way that the people speak and the press writes that what is this law, the language of the law, which is the language of lawyers, this language of birds and butterflies, which no one understands. I will read you a quote that characterizes what exists and is based on how our legislators and advocates of the law have viewed our constitution through interpretation and what they have done with the Constitution. Quote: "Everyone has the right to concentrate in non-profit organisations and associations. Only Estonian citizens may belong to political parties. " Yes, indeed, I quoted the Constitution to you. Do you think this is the norm? Unfortunately, it doesn't. Because we're going to adopt one of the lower acts, we're going to break the law of the party and we're going to read it, I think it was § 5, which says that non-citizens can hear it too. So, what's going on? The same classic thing has happened that our constitution is being chewed and changed by various interpretations of its norms into nullity or inability to work. Because some lower act regulates it differently than the Constitution provides. The Ülo Vooglaid has once said very wise words that we must be honest with people and, if we want to change the constitution, we must honestly say to the people: we want to change, this legal relationship is no longer working, we will reverse it, and then we will put another norm to the referendum. For example, in this same example, it could be said that political parties may belong to everyone who lives here, and we put it into the Constitutional norm, we put it to the vote on the Constitution, the people can decide. Or that the family consists of boxes and cats, but not of man and woman and his children, ascendant and descending offspring. Let's be honest about this. But no, we're doing things like this around the corner, as long as people don't know what's going on. I'll read you another quote: "It's forbidden to /... / parties whose /... / action is /... / contrary to the law providing for criminal liability. " Period. Where did it come from? The Constitution. What does this rule say in a constitution that ends with a point? He says that parties who have committed a crime and whose conduct is contrary to the law providing for criminal liability are prohibited. But what about us? We've got them moving on. Not only will they continue to act, but they will also be able to rule. How such a fact affects our social order, our attitude toward our country and our government is not likely to be difficult for us to imagine. The fundamental division between the people and the country comes from where such things are carried out. I was able to study law at the university under the hands of someone named Igor Gräzin. You all know him, you all know him. True, my studio started in Soviet times, but I can say I graduated with a diploma with three puppies on it, so at a better time. With regard to Igor, we had a very respectable attitude at the university, which is why Igor Gräzin is the youngest law professor in the Soviet Union, wise as the devil. Not knowing whether it was true or not, it made us proud and it was good for us to learn under him, because his lectures were interesting, meaningful, and sometimes funny. They weren't boring, they were nice. Igor Gräzin has also been sitting in this hall. And at one point, he, who has strong legal knowledge, was very well hit by what's going on here. He caught it, which he probably knew earlier, which really means the Constitution, what is the meaning of the regulations governing the governance of the state, where parties, parties have been given extraordinary power and power. They are non-profit organisations, like housing associations, but they are granted a state right of responsibility and have a special law, a party law. This means that political parties must be treated with extraordinarily careful and extraordinarily precise; there cannot be any slippage, because the fish is starting to rot out of its head. Parties that run the country, if they move wrong, against our wishes and laws, it also rots our country. And here comes the attitude of the people. In short, going back to Igor Gräzin. Gräzin, being in this hall, discovered at a good moment that some strange metamorphosis was taking place in relation to laws and constitutions. In 2014, I believe that he was probably also in contact with his own party, including with the leaders, he discovered that for some reason the principle, with which political parties were given considerable attention and the sword of Damocles was placed on their head long ago, was suddenly lost in the Penal Code, which provided for the possibility of the party having committed a crime according to the Constitution. I even quite sincerely believe that he paid attention to this fact even when certain political circles began to implement the matter, but, well, probably, his position in his party at that time was such that it was not reasonably possible to block it. We can only now think about why the composition and government of Riigikogu XII was then the first and the second government of Rõvas went that way, that they decided to eliminate this rule from the Penal Code, which warned and kept parties from committing crimes. However, in any case, the fact is that the 2014 Penal Code reform, which entered into force in 2015, ceased to be the possibility that the party that committed the crime could be eradicated. He also addressed the Chancellor of Justice separately, asking what it was, how it was possible. How is it possible that § 64 of the Constitution provides that the powers of a member of the Riigikogu will end before the deadline when the sentence condemning him enters into force, the same rules are repeated by the Act on the status of a member of the Riigikogu. This is an unambiguous rule that precludes the participation of a legal person, a party convicted of a crime, in the work of the Riigikogu. Estonia's legal order no longer contains it for some reason. I, too, later, after getting to it, made inquiries to lawyers and received an answer, which is very simple and logical. Indeed, by the law amending the Penal Code and other related acts, it was decided to repeal § 46 of the General Penal Code "mandatory winding-up of a legal person" and consequently to repeal all the paragraphs of the Special Part of the Penal Code, and there are sanctions for the compulsory winding-up of a legal person. The aforementioned Act entered into force on 1 January 2015 and by this amendment the legislator waived the power delegated to it by Article 48 (3) of the Constitution to determine the criminal offences for which the court was ordered to punish a legal person, including a political party, by a compulsory winding-up, as a result of a criminal investigation. A conscious choice, a fully aware choice. When this reform was carried out, it was justified by the fact that you see, people, that the forced termination is not by its very nature lost, that we still have him in civil law. And with it again they deceived the people, double-crossed the people. First of all, one of the rules of the Constitution was essentially rendered incapacitated, the other being that it is really quite difficult for a simple person to explain what the difference is between civil law, criminal law and public law. All these procedures follow a different path and, after all, in the case of criminal penalties, civil proceedings do not allow the law to be understood or the code of civil procedure to be used. And there will be contradictions, different contradictions. At the end of the day, the question is, "Wait a while, whether we will not be able to convict a misbehaved legal person, including a party, after all, we have a law on the general part of the Civil Code, we also have a party law. Once again, these are civil laws. Yes, indeed, we have a formal opportunity left. But with this formal opportunity, it is now the fact that, even at this point in time, we will be able to carry out liquidation only and only if, for example, a party or a legal person literally carries out a coup, or if the Government of the Republic takes this step to the court in order to put an end to a party that has behaved improperly. But you know that's impossible. It's a formal rule, which, although prescribed by some way, is essentially impossible. It can never be that a minister or government is turning to a court to dismantle a party. Then it's just a political game. In principle, we also have the possibility that the court will do so on its own initiative, but I cannot imagine that either, especially in a situation where these penal sanctions have been deliberately removed from the Penal Code. And I stress: we do not know for what reason. We may think about what happened in 2013, 2014, that the political circles saw that this sword needed to be taken from its head. As I said, this is a very unfair and very wrong signal. And that is why the Conservative Party has come up with a proposal to reinstate this situation in order, in a sense, to create a certain level of discipline in our legislation. That is also why we have come up with this bill. The government, in its opinion on the same draft, has said that it has been very clear that, by its very nature, we are not doing anything new. Moreover, we are, in fact, only restoring the situation where we are introducing these necessary general and specific rules, these necessary types of punishment, so that forced termination is simply possible. In fact, since this is a first reading, it is, in principle, possible at any moment when we have introduced these standards to clarify all the situations so that there is no excessive fear for those parties that are currently under attack. But, as Jaanus Karilaid said in our committee during this conversation, no one needs to feel fear, because even if we restore the situation that has been taken away from us now, there is no retroactive force on this law. So what's gone is gone. But for the future, it would give a very strong and very good signal. And I think it would make sense to support it for all of you. That's the story. Thank you!

ParlaMint-EE_2021-04-14_U602	Our eighth item is the draft law amending the Penal Code, initiated by the Group of the Conservative People's Party of Estonia, with number 339. At first reading, the Anti Poolamets will make a presentation on behalf of the initiators. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-04-14_U603	[[Remote connection]] Envoys! The draft law amending the Penal Code proposed by the EKRE aims to increase the protection of minors against sexual abuse. Estonia is currently a minority among the Member States of the European Union under the current Penal Code, where an adult can have sexual relations with a minor of at least 14 years of age. Given the role of sexual offences against minors among all sexual offences, it is necessary to increase the protection of minors against the type of crime which has a long-term impact on children's development and mentality. Sexual crimes affecting minors have made up a stable majority of about 85 to 90% of all registered sexual crimes. In 2020, 222 sexual crimes were committed against minors. In the first quarter of this year, 70 sexual crimes were committed against minors. By amending § 145 of the Penal Code, a person of at least 21 years of age must not have sexual intercourse or engage in any other sexual activity involving persons under 16 years of age. The aim of the amendment is to increase the protection of children under 16 years of age from sexual offences. According to the current law, for example, a 50-year-old man or woman is eligible for intercourse with a 15-year-old child if this is done with the consent of both parties. However, this cannot be considered tolerable given the vulnerability of children, the manipulation of adults and vulnerability. As a recent example, the reasons for the football club Nõmme Kalju's coach, Getulio Aurelio Fredo, for being in a relationship with a person under 14 years of age, startled Estonian society. Today, he has also been subjected to a suspicion of a crime. However, there has been a consistent press response to such cases, recalling the doubts expressed in August 2020 for the coach of the Ihase Horse School. On the basis of a number of other restrictions which impose a minimum age of 21 years, it is also reasonable to provide for a minimum age of 21 years in this respect in order to have a ratio below 16 years of age. Among the neighbouring countries of Estonia, the age limit for sexual self-determination is 16 years in Finland, Latvia and Lithuania. In addition, the same age limit applies in Spain, Great Britain, Norway, Belgium, the Netherlands. In the United States, the age limit is 16 years in the vast majority of states. Seven states have a minimum age of 17 years and twelve states have 18 years of age. The amendment of § 178 of the Penal Code may not use a person under 16 years of age in the manufacture, acquisition or preservation of a pornographic or erotic image, writing or other work or reproduction, transfer, display or otherwise making available to another person. This section, too, has a minimum age of 14 years under the law in force, but for the above reasons, it is reasonable to increase the minimum age to 16 years. Under the current law, the maximum penalty is three years of imprisonment, which is not proportionate to the seriousness of the crime. Consequently, it is reasonable to extend the maximum penalty to 10 years imprisonment. In Latvia, for example, the maximum penalty for committing a similar crime is 15 years in prison. The amendments to § 1781 (1) of the Penal Code render punishable the submission of a meeting to a person under 16 years of age or the conclusion of a meeting agreement with him and the preparatory act of the meeting. The amendments to paragraph 1781 (1) establish a maximum penalty rate of 10 years. It makes sense to raise prison sentences when committing repeated crimes. We aim to give the judiciary greater discretion, if necessary, to impose longer term prison sentences. What is the purpose of the sentence? Restoring justice, of course. At the same time, the aim is to reduce the number of crimes. Our conviction is that...... for a long time... [[I can't hear you.]] And I am very pleased that there is now a cross-party consensus to move forward by changing the age line. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-04-14_U657	Act amending the Basic School and Upper Secondary School Act. In principle, it is understandable that the crisis requires flexibility, solutions and the correction of the corresponding laws. When the Ministry just started to say that it is probably necessary to ensure flexibility in the conduct of the examinations, I think that there were no people in the Commission who did not understand it or would have directly opposed it. Everyone was in favour of looking at how the system could be coordinated with the established situation. However, there are a number of weak points in this bill that we are dealing with today and which will probably be adopted. We have great respect for the principle of the question: what is the exam? The test in its original sense is a natural part of the learning process. The student learns and, through the exam, receives his or her parents, his or her teacher, his or her school, but also the country's feedback on whether what has been offered through the school system has been memorized, learned or not. If the answer is no, the system will receive a signal that nothing will work, and the reason for failure may not necessarily be at student level; the problem may be in other areas, such as materials, school, teacher, methodology. There could be a whole line of reasons. But in order to come to an analysis of how the education system operates, exams are needed as a control of knowledge and learned material. Lately, the minister hears a lot of psychological explanation that we don't take the exam, the students have a lot of stress. Agreed. Psychological status is very important. But it has nothing to do with whether the examination needs to be passed or whether it should be revoked. Stress is about how we conduct exams, how we understand final exams as a society, and how we explain their need to students. When the adult job is done, the child's test stress is also minimal. Hence the answer to the question whether we need exams. Yes, we certainly need them, they are needed by the student himself, the teacher, the school, the Ministry of Education and Research, the parent and the state as a whole. The other thing is that we have a crisis. During the crisis, the system should be flexible: additional time, additional space, possibilities to change the schedule of examinations. And indeed, if the situation goes completely out of control, the Minister may be given the authority to consider as a last resort, or as an opportunity, when assessing the situation, in order to untie the exams. Everything I just listed was originally in the draft sent by the Ministry of Education and Research. But then HTM suddenly started to improve its own draft and essentially changed the logic of the draft, to untie [the exams from graduating] anyway, despite the fact that the situation seems to be getting better and not worse. In principle, untie the completion of the exams. And what is important, [to do this] is not under the responsibility of the minister, but to transfer responsibility to the Riigikogu. These questions that I listed earlier are very substantive things that are no longer in this draft: exams as part of the logical learning process and ministerial responsibility for the whole process. It was removed from the draft on a ministerial proposal. Let's take a look at some technical stuff. There's a lot of problems here too, so let me list them. Firstly, the draft, which will be completed during the crisis, at the last minute, will be sent to the Commission, asked for its swift processing and will be amended immediately by the Ministry. What is that? It is unprepared during the crisis to send a draft and immediately start writing amendments to its own draft. Secondly, the fact that the Minister is sending his proposal for amendment, there is a debate in the Commission, the Members of the Commission realise that they are discussing the Minister's proposal, and the next day this proposal will be introduced by a majority in the form of a proposal from the Committee on Culture. What is that? This, again, is unprepared. Legally, it was as if everything was correct, essentially not so much. Thirdly, as soon as we deal with this bill, this law will still enter into force once the first exam is over. Perhaps formally, the same Monday Estonian exam is still compulsory. What is that? Unprepared again, poorly done work. I'm asking for more time!

In addition, the investment aid granted to local authorities is EUR 30 million, i.e. the maximum amount of the aid is calculated for each municipality. The basic amount per municipality is EUR 50000, the rest being divided by the number of inhabitants. The purpose of the investment aid is to promote local employment and the economy. This money allows cities and municipalities to make the necessary investments as they see fit, such as improving ventilation systems, building and renovating buildings, dismantling old buildings, purchasing IT security-compliant servers.
